For each amendment, the exact heading is `### Controlled Amendment — <amendmentID>`, and `sectionDigest = SHA256("QINAO-K3-AMENDMENT-V1\0" || uint64be(len(amendmentIDBytes)) || amendmentIDBytes || uint64be(len(sectionBytes)) || sectionBytes)`, where `sectionBytes` starts immediately after that heading and ends immediately before the next equal-or-shallower heading. The digest is stored only in the ledger manifest, never inside its own section. Fixture families are the accepting/expected and rejecting columns' semicolon-delimited cells in left-to-right order. Their IDs are respectively `amendmentID + ".accept." + twoDigitOrdinal` and `amendmentID + ".reject." + twoDigitOrdinal`; `.accept.` means a positive conformance oracle and may therefore require an expected typed denial such as `sent_or_unknown`, rather than acceptance of an external action. A fixture's cases have IDs `fixtureID + ".case." + twoDigitOrdinal`. Slash/pipe prose is never parsed to discover cases: the reviewed case-ID constant below explicitly expands every parameter variant, and every unlisted single-case fixture has exactly `.case.01`. The checker requires a non-zero matched count and exact typed oracle for **every case ID**, not merely one case per family. It requires exactly the six ordered amendment IDs, one unique bounded section each, exact planned fixture records, and digest equality. Missing/extra/duplicate/reordered IDs, a keyword-only occurrence, wrong wave/slice/suite/case assignment, removed clause, a flipped expected outcome, or an always-accept/always-reject implementation fails; mutations exercise each class for every row.

`OracleOutcomeV1` is a closed typed object, not a Boolean or free-form reason. Its exact schema is:

```text
schemaVersion = 1
fixtureClass = acceptingExpected | rejecting
preState = {stateFixtureID, canonicalStateDigest}
transitionDisposition =
  committedExpected | rejectedNoMutation | idempotentlyReopened |
  lostRaceReopensWinner | indeterminatePreserved
postState = {stateFixtureID, canonicalStateDigest}
rowDeltas[] =
  {rowScopeSchemaID, ownerID, rowKind, canonicalKey, keyDigest,
   before = absent | present(stateFixtureID, stateDigest),
   after = absent | present(stateFixtureID, stateDigest)}
activeHead = notApplicable(reasonCode) | unchanged(headDigest) | moved(beforeDigest, afterDigest) |
  casLost(expectedDigest, actualDigest)
terminalState = notApplicable(reasonCode) | absent |
  failed(reasonCode) | authenticatedTerminal(outcomeDigest) |
  sentOrUnknown(reasonCode) | indeterminateQuarantined(reasonCode)
publicationState = notApplicable(reasonCode) | blocked | nonpublishable |
  publishable(authorityReceiptDigest)
proposalIDState = notApplicable(reasonCode) | nilValue | nonNil(valueDigest)
resultIDState = notApplicable(reasonCode) | nilValue | nonNil(valueDigest)
generationDelta = exact(signedInt64) | notApplicable(reasonCode)
successorDelta = exact(signedInt64) | notApplicable(reasonCode)
observedBytes = exact(sentUInt64, receivedUInt64) | notApplicable(reasonCode)
mechanismStartCount = exact(UInt64) | notApplicable(reasonCode)
oracleReason = none | typed(reasonCode)
```

All functional notation in this section is schema shorthand only; the wire is singular. Every tagged-union instance is exactly the two-key JSON object `{"payload":[...],"tag":"<variant>"}`. A zero-argument variant has `payload:[]`; arguments appear in the written left-to-right order and nested objects use their declared exact fields. No variant-specific key, omitted payload, scalar shorthand, JSON `null`, or alternate tag spelling is accepted. Every tagged-union descriptor is exactly `{"kind":"taggedUnion","variants":[{"payload_types":[...],"tag":"<variant>"},...]}` in declared variant order. Thus `O<T>` expands to absent with an empty `payload_types` array followed by present with `[T]`; `E[x,...]` is not a union instance but the JSON string value selected from descriptor `{"kind":"enum","values":["x",...]}`. `D`, `U`, `I`, and `B` expand respectively to descriptor bytes `{"format":"sha256-lower-hex","kind":"digest"}`, `{"bits":64,"kind":"uint"}`, `{"bits":64,"kind":"int"}`, and `{"kind":"bool"}`. `S[p,n]` expands to `{"kind":"utf8String","maxBytes":n,"patternID":p}` and is validated over its exact UTF-8 preimage before canonical JSON escaping. A `reasonCode` is a JSON string that must occur in the owning signed case binding's sorted `allowed_reason_codes`; it is never caller prose.

The cross-language golden instance vectors, with no trailing LF, are: `29 / 865a6119d0a712998a926673a38754eb039d9e79fdee009b8e2cd958430bb384 / {"payload":[],"tag":"absent"}`; `29 / a54d66cfebe3dab5b5c43c5b4f56a47cf83bd7d933d760747071980382f91620 / {"payload":[0],"tag":"exact"}`; and `37 / 06fc6c4694f82da68f3afda9d71ba96539fba676b61463c9f46bc397187e6734 / {"payload":[{"x":1}],"tag":"present"}`. Python and Swift each construct and parse all descriptor/instance variants independently, assert these byte-count/SHA-256 vectors, and mutation-reject swapped payload positions, absent payload, extra key, `null`, scalar shorthand, reordered descriptor variants, and a reason string absent from the case binding.

Every set-like string array is sorted by unsigned UTF-8 bytes and duplicate-free; registry schemas sort by `row_scope_schema_id`, state fixtures by `state_fixture_id`, outcomes by `case_id`, and aggregate rows plus `rowDeltas` by `(rowScopeSchemaID,keyDigest)` under that same byte ordering. Descriptor field arrays and enum/tagged-union variants instead preserve the exact declared order below and reject reordering. Every digest is 64 lowercase hex; integers reject overflow, negative unsigned values, floats, strings, and defaults. All keys above are mandatory in that order-independent JSON object, and only the declared tagged variants are legal. A case whose contract requires zero bytes or zero starts must use `exact(0,0)` or `exact(0)`, never `notApplicable`. State fixture, registry, and outcome objects use strict UTF-8 compact sorted-key JSON, duplicate keys forbidden, no trailing LF.

The fixed `f5901898684229cb56a61afecb8ea52cc3d52b2b` baseline has **zero** amendment-eligible physical row schemas. Its Swift EventLog v2 tables cannot express the Attempt/Budget/Provider/K4 contracts, its integrity sidecar is optional, its Rust EventLog shape is a divergent frozen writer, Artifact Mesh belongs to another owner and contains a randomized storage envelope, `sovereign_tokens` is schema-only, and the K4 durable lifecycle is still `approved_missing`. Task 4 therefore must not claim that a future SQL table, actor map, `ExtensionSlice`, or serializer already exists. Instead, W0 freezes one non-production, non-authoritative validation vocabulary named `OracleRowScopeRegistryV1`; it creates no Owner Ledger owner, writer, table, state store, ExtensionGate entry, or W0 schema fixture. Its exact `physical_baseline` is `{approved_spec: <the complete five-field tuple>, eligible_physical_row_scope_ids: [], disposition: logical_evidence_only_future_binding_required}`. Any non-empty W0 physical allowlist, use of current EventLog/Artifact Mesh/audit/schema-only K4 rows as amendment truth, or claim that a logical schema is already realized fails.

The registry is one closed object with exactly `schema_version = 1`, `registry_id = qinao-k3-amendment-oracle-row-scope-registry-v1`, `physical_baseline`, sorted `row_scope_schemas[9]`, six declaration-ordered `case_input_schemas`, sorted `row_invariants`, sorted `production_event_contracts`, and six declaration-ordered `amendment_relation_laws`. Every schema entry has exactly `row_scope_schema_id`, `schema_version = 1`, `authority_owner_id`, `logical_row_kind`, sorted non-empty `allowed_amendment_ids`, `realization_wave`, ordered non-empty `key_fields`, ordered non-empty `state_fields`, sorted non-empty `row_invariant_ids`, and `delta_law = keyedBeforeAfterExact`. A case-input-schema record has exactly `{case_input_schema_id,amendment_id,fields}` and preserves the field declaration order in the table below. A row-invariant record has exactly `{invariant_id,subject_row_scope_schema_ids,predicate_version,golden_vector_table_digest}` with its singleton subject array; a relation-law record has exactly `{relation_law_id,amendment_id,subject_row_roles,allowed_event_kinds,predicate_version,golden_vector_table_digest}` with the exact declaration-ordered role tuple below and an unsigned-UTF-8 sorted/unique event-kind subset from the table immediately below. Both predicate versions are exactly `1`, and the table digest is domain-separated over the independently implemented canonical golden-vector table. `logical_row_kind` is byte-identical to that entry's `row_scope_schema_id`; no alias or physical table name is legal. Each field descriptor is exactly `{name,type}`. `A1...A6` below alias the six amendment IDs in table order only for display; canonical registry bytes contain their full IDs.

`production_event_contracts[]` contains exactly these fifteen sorted records; canonical registry bytes use the literal token in column 1 and expand every other column into the exact record `{event_kind,boundary_kind,symbol_role,owner_rule,time_rule}`. `boundary_kind` is `notApplicable(decisionOnly) | present(<one of the nine boundary kinds>)`; `symbol_role` is `controlDecisionSymbol | mappingSnapshotSymbol | mappingWriterSymbol | mappingSerializerSymbol | mappingLifecycleSymbol`; `owner_rule` is `amendmentDecisionOwner | selectedMappingStorageOwner | selectedMappingAuthorityOwner | mappingLifecycleOwner`; and `time_rule` is `notApplicable(noGovernedTime) | requiredFromCaseGrantTimingAndBinding`. “Selected” means the mapping uniquely selected by the matched invocation/snapshot and its `evidence_owner.mapping_side`: current/replay rows select the highest active mapping, `backwardPrior` selects the immediate predecessor, and `backwardCurrent` selects the current mapping. Only `requiredFromCaseGrantTimingAndBinding` additionally requires the complete `a2_timing_binding` record, mandatory grant-row scope/key, GT-selected phase/cardinality, and causal pairing defined by the trace contract; mapping side alone never requires timing.

| event_kind | boundary_kind | symbol_role | owner_rule | time_rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `a1.rebaseDecision` | `notApplicable(decisionOnly)` | `controlDecisionSymbol` | `amendmentDecisionOwner` | `notApplicable(noGovernedTime)` |
| `a2.recoveryOrderDecision` | `notApplicable(decisionOnly)` | `controlDecisionSymbol` | `amendmentDecisionOwner` | `requiredFromCaseGrantTimingAndBinding` |
| `a3.ownerLossDecision` | `notApplicable(decisionOnly)` | `controlDecisionSymbol` | `amendmentDecisionOwner` | `notApplicable(noGovernedTime)` |
| `a4.egressDecision` | `notApplicable(decisionOnly)` | `controlDecisionSymbol` | `amendmentDecisionOwner` | `notApplicable(noGovernedTime)` |
| `a5.costDecision` | `notApplicable(decisionOnly)` | `controlDecisionSymbol` | `amendmentDecisionOwner` | `notApplicable(noGovernedTime)` |
| `a6.tokenValidationDecision` | `notApplicable(decisionOnly)` | `controlDecisionSymbol` | `amendmentDecisionOwner` | `notApplicable(noGovernedTime)` |
| `physical.coldOpen` | `present(coldOpen)` | `mappingLifecycleSymbol` | `mappingLifecycleOwner` | `notApplicable(noGovernedTime)` |
| `physical.committedChange` | `present(committedChange)` | `mappingWriterSymbol` | `selectedMappingStorageOwner` | `notApplicable(noGovernedTime)` |
| `physical.denial` | `present(denial)` | `mappingWriterSymbol` | `selectedMappingStorageOwner` | `notApplicable(noGovernedTime)` |
| `physical.migration` | `present(migration)` | `mappingLifecycleSymbol` | `mappingLifecycleOwner` | `notApplicable(noGovernedTime)` |
| `physical.migrationTransaction` | `present(migrationTransaction)` | `mappingLifecycleSymbol` | `mappingLifecycleOwner` | `notApplicable(noGovernedTime)` |
| `physical.query` | `present(query)` | `mappingSnapshotSymbol` | `selectedMappingStorageOwner` | `notApplicable(noGovernedTime)` |
| `physical.serializer` | `present(serializer)` | `mappingSerializerSymbol` | `selectedMappingAuthorityOwner` | `notApplicable(noGovernedTime)` |
| `physical.teardown` | `present(teardown)` | `mappingLifecycleSymbol` | `mappingLifecycleOwner` | `notApplicable(noGovernedTime)` |
| `physical.transactionAttempt` | `present(transactionAttempt)` | `mappingWriterSymbol` | `selectedMappingStorageOwner` | `notApplicable(noGovernedTime)` |

A1–A6 each allow its one same-prefix decision token plus all nine `physical.*` tokens; canonical `allowed_event_kinds` contains those exact ten values in unsigned UTF-8 order. Runtime owner/symbol identity is not a free table string: decision owner/symbol equal the amendment's controlled decision owner/boundary; snapshot/writer events use the selected mapping's exact storage owner and symbols; serializer uses its selected mapping's exact authority owner; lifecycle uses the static projection/migration contract owner/symbol and the exact prior/current field split above. The event's `evidence_owner` and `mapping_side`, matched complete invocation, resolved snapshot, and applicable selected mapping(s) must all imply the same value. Each event `owner_id` equals that derived value. Query/denial/attempt/change/migration kinds that embed a decoded production receipt must additionally equal its decoded owner; serializer derives its authority owner from its complete mapping-bound preimage and does not invent a nonexistent decoded receipt; teardown/cold-open use the static lifecycle contract owner. Every signed case-fact schedule selects an exact declaration-ordered event sequence from only its amendment subset. The registry root, Oracle selection, external verifier, runner, Swift golden tables, and post-embed checker independently reconstruct this table and sequence. Unknown/missing/extra event, wrong amendment subset, event↔boundary/symbol/owner/time/mapping-side mismatch, or a re-signed catalog with a changed token fails. Mutation changes only a syntactically valid owner ID or mapping side while preserving every other byte/root and must fail.

The descriptor algebra is closed under scalar, union, record, and array construction. In addition to `D = {kind:digest,format:sha256-lower-hex}`, `U = {kind:uint,bits:64}`, `I = {kind:int,bits:64}`, `B = {kind:bool}`, `S[p,n] = {kind:utf8String,patternID:p,maxBytes:n}`, `O<T> = {kind:taggedUnion,variants:[absent,present(T)]}`, and `E[x,...] = {kind:enum,values:[x,...]}`, it has exactly `R[(name1,T1),...] = {kind:record,fields:[{name:name1,type:T1},...]}` and `A<T;order;unique> = {kind:array,item_type:T,order_by:[...],unique_by:[...]}` under the literal JSON wire above. Record fields preserve declaration order. Array `order_by` and `unique_by` are non-empty arrays of exact field paths or the one admitted computed token `canonicalFactValueBytes(fact_value)`; unknown/computed expressions are forbidden. No map, tuple, unbounded string, `any`, recursive descriptor, or omitted constraint exists.

The display aliases used by all six case schemas are exact: `V = S[k3-case-variant-v1,512]`; `RC = S[k3-reason-code-v1,128]`; `FID = S[k3-case-fact-id-v1,128]`; `FSID = S[k3-case-fact-schema-id-v1,128]`; `FEV = S[k3-case-fact-enum-value-v1,128]`; `FV = bool(B) | uint(U) | digest(D) | enum(FSID,FEV)`; `F = A<R[(fact_id,FID),(fact_value,FV)]; [fact_id,canonicalFactValueBytes(fact_value)]; [fact_id]>`; `GT = success(D,U,U,U) | expiredBeforeClaim(D,U,U) | expiredAfterClaim(D,U,U,U) | terminalDenied(D,U,U,RC) | revokedBeforeClaim(D,U,U) | revokedAfterClaim(D,U,U,U)`, where every variant begins `(grantTimeDomainDigest,grantDeadlineTick,...)`; `AP = notApplicable(D) | applicable(D,D,D,D,GT)` in exact `(applicabilityProofDigest,actionRequestDigest,materializedRequestDigest,grantRequestDigest,grantTiming)` order; and `EG = notApplicable(RC) | customExact() | pccOpaque()`. All union aliases use the universal `payload_types`/`payload` wire and declaration order. These aliases are plan typography only: every `case_input_schemas.fields[].type` contains the fully expanded descriptor object, so no alias token can enter canonical registry bytes.

The five mandatory descriptor golden bytes are: `V = 69 / 78e8e96018df2e30cb1b6c9375f119b8f440a97a143a4628870bccf383b0cdf1`; `GT = 1035 / 660788a8fd18b0c1d8e59c013fb8d8e0d52cbf1b0fddfa79a75b78d8e3b7248c`; `AP = 1382 / 3526458b325e997c2b2ef12d177936913d715b84540f8d193b19d2affbec3c6c`; `EG = 226 / bbfbdadec43288e7ba9363d95e2b6bd80c94261fe9e5469cb19256b45ea8ac6f`; and `F = 680 / cceaa180d41e4889143a75a1861356c9953cc9524daa9898ea6444263c59fa72`, where each pair is `canonical byte count / raw SHA-256`. Independent Python and Swift tests construct those descriptors rather than pasting the digest, byte-compare the canonical encoding, and mutation-reject record-field reorder, array `order_by`/`unique_by` removal or swap, duplicate fact ID, union variant or payload-type reorder/arity change, string pattern/max drift, and an AP/GT/EG tag or payload substitution.

`S[json-number-lexeme-v1,128]` accepts exactly ASCII regex `-?(0|[1-9][0-9]*)(\.[0-9]+)?([eE][+-]?[0-9]+)?`, no whitespace, and retains those exact UTF-8 bytes; semantic range/class is checked separately. The five case-input IDs `k3-case-variant-v1`, `k3-reason-code-v1`, `k3-case-fact-id-v1`, `k3-case-fact-schema-id-v1`, and `k3-case-fact-enum-value-v1` accept exactly ASCII `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:-]*` within their descriptor byte bound; `k3-case-variant-v1` additionally forbids `:`. The production-observation IDs are separate and exact: `k3-observation-nonce-v1` accepts exactly `[0-9a-f]{32}`, represents 128 bits from the platform CSPRNG, and must be unique across every wrapper invocation and present child observation in one aggregate run; `k3-production-event-kind-v1` accepts `[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9._:-]{0,127}` and must additionally be one member of the declaration-ordered per-amendment event-kind allowlist; `k3-owner-id-v1` and `k3-row-scope-schema-id-v1` accept `[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9._:-]{0,127}`; and `k3-symbol-id-v1` accepts `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9._:+-]{0,255}`. These five production patterns have independent golden accept/reject vectors; a syntactically valid but unknown event kind, repeated nonce, uppercase/short/long nonce, non-ASCII scalar, or over-bound identifier fails before trace-law evaluation. Every key/state/object contains every listed field exactly once and no other field; optional semantic values use `O<T>` and JSON `null` is never a value. `reasonOrOutcomeDigest` and every other reason/evidence field is a digest of its separately governed typed value, never free-form prose.

| row-scope schema ID | owner / first realization | allowed amendments | Exact ordered logical key fields | Exact ordered logical state fields |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `qinao.oracle.k3.attempt-rebase-control.v1` | `state.k3-control-nucleus` / W2 | A1 | `workspaceScopeDigest:D, workUnitDigest:D, predecessorAttemptDigest:D, rebaseRequestDigest:D` | `phase:E[rebasePending,headInstalled,activationPending,usable,terminalDenied], activeHeadDigest:D, successorAttemptDigest:O<D>, attemptGeneration:U, cumulativeUseDigest:D, visitedStateDigest:D, rightsDigest:D, ceilingsDigest:D, deadlineDigest:D, bootSessionDigest:D, policyEpoch:U, deletionEpoch:U, revocationEpoch:U, revision:U` |
| `qinao.oracle.k3.budget-use.v1` | `state.k3-control-nucleus` / W2 | A1, A2, A5, A6 | `turnOperationDigest:D, attemptDigest:D, leaseDigest:D, budgetSubject:E[attemptRebase,providerCancel,providerTerminalQuery,maxCostWarrant,profileTokenObservation], subjectOrdinal:U, subjectRequestDigest:D` | `generation:U, revision:U, inputTokens:U, outputTokens:U, reasoningTokens:U, toolUnits:U, cacheUnits:U, requestUnits:U, reservedMaxCost:U, observedCost:O<U>, latestBudgetUseReceiptDigest:O<D>, terminal:E[open,closed,indeterminate], visitedStateDigest:D, policyEpoch:U, deletionEpoch:U, revocationEpoch:U` |
| `qinao.oracle.k3.provider-recovery-action.v1` | `state.k3-control-nucleus` / W2 | A2 | `turnOperationDigest:D, branchDigest:D, originalPermitDigest:D, actionKind:E[cancel,terminalQuery], ordinal:U` | `actionState = notApplicable(applicabilityProofDigest:D, ownerEpoch:U, policyEpoch:U, deletionEpoch:U, revocationEpoch:U, revision:U) \| applicable(applicabilityProofDigest:D, phase:E[prepared,pending,armed,handedOff,sentOrUnknown,authenticatedTerminal,indeterminateQuarantined], materializedRequestDigest:D, grantDigest:O<D>, budgetUseDigest:O<D>, terminalObservationDigest:O<D>, ownerEpoch:U, policyEpoch:U, deletionEpoch:U, revocationEpoch:U, revision:U)` |
| `qinao.oracle.k3.provider-egress-marker.v1` | `state.k3-control-nucleus` / W5 | A2, A4 | `turnOperationDigest:D, branchDigest:D, providerProfileDigest:D, egressKind:E[customExact,pccOpaque]` | `marker:E[customTransportTaskCreatedFalse,customTransportTaskCreatedTrue,pccRespondEnteredFalse,pccRespondEnteredTrue,notApplicable], ownerEpoch:U, policyEpoch:U, deletionEpoch:U, revocationEpoch:U, revision:U` |
| `qinao.oracle.k3.provider-terminal-event.v1` | `state.k3-control-nucleus` / W2 | A2, A3, A4 | `turnOperationDigest:D, branchDigest:D, eventDigest:D` | `terminal:E[open,failed,authenticatedTerminal,sentOrUnknown,indeterminateQuarantined], reasonOrOutcomeDigest:O<D>, proposalIDDigest:O<D>, resultIDDigest:O<D>, publication:E[blocked,nonpublishable,publishable], authorityReceiptDigest:O<D>, generation:U, successorCount:U, observedSentBytes:U, observedReceivedBytes:U, mechanismStartCount:U, ownerEpoch:U, revision:U` |
| `qinao.oracle.k3.profile-token-observation.v1` | `state.k3-control-nucleus` / W2 | A6 | `attemptDigest:D, profileDigest:D, geometryDigest:D, materializedRequestDigest:D, leaseDigest:D, grantDigest:D` | `observedToken = invalid(inputDigest:D) \| parsed(U), safeIntegerBound:U, validatorCeiling:U, disposition:E[pending,accepted,rejected], revision:U` |
| `qinao.oracle.k4.rebase-grant-reservation.v1` | `sovereign.k4-durable-lifecycle` / W5 | A1 | `predecessorAttemptDigest:D, successorAttemptDigest:D, reservationRequestDigest:D` | `grantDigest:D, lifecycle:E[reservedInactive,activationPending,usable,terminalDenied,revoked,expired], rightsDigest:D, ceilingsDigest:D, deadlineDigest:D, minimumPolicyEpoch:U, minimumDeletionEpoch:U, minimumRevocationEpoch:U, anchoredK3Root:D, revision:U` |
| `qinao.oracle.k4.provider-action-grant.v1` | `sovereign.k4-durable-lifecycle` / W5 | A2 | `turnOperationDigest:D, branchDigest:D, actionKind:E[cancel,terminalQuery], ordinal:U, grantRequestDigest:D` | `grantDigest:D, lifecycle:E[reservedInactive,claimed,spent,terminalDenied,revoked,expired], materializedRequestDigest:D, anchoredK3Root:D, timeDomainDigest:D, deadlineTick:U, claimedAtTick:O<U>, spentAtTick:O<U>, terminalObservedAtTick:O<U>, minimumPolicyEpoch:U, minimumDeletionEpoch:U, minimumRevocationEpoch:U, revision:U` |
| `qinao.oracle.k4.max-cost-warrant.v1` | `sovereign.k4-durable-lifecycle` / W5 | A5 | `turnOperationDigest:D, branchDigest:D, warrantRequestDigest:D, profileDigest:D, priceRevisionDigest:D` | `warrantDigest:D, lifecycle:E[reservedInactive,active,spent,terminalDenied,revoked,expired,indeterminate], reservedInputCost:U, reservedOutputCost:U, reservedReasoningCost:U, reservedToolCost:U, reservedCacheCost:U, reservedRequestCost:U, reservedMaxCost:U, observedCost:O<U>, downwardCredit:U, quotaBoundsDigest:D, platformBoundsDigest:D, anchoredK3Root:D, revision:U` |

`K3_ORACLE_ROW_INVARIANTS_V1` is not an unsigned prose hook. It is the following exact one-to-one schema mapping; each schema's `row_invariant_ids` is the singleton shown, and the registry's sorted `row_invariants` array contains exactly these nine IDs:

```text
qinao.oracle.k3.attempt-rebase-control.v1 -> qinao.oracle.invariant.attempt-rebase-control.v1
qinao.oracle.k3.budget-use.v1 -> qinao.oracle.invariant.budget-use-monotonic.v1
qinao.oracle.k3.profile-token-observation.v1 -> qinao.oracle.invariant.profile-token-observation.v1
qinao.oracle.k3.provider-egress-marker.v1 -> qinao.oracle.invariant.provider-egress-marker.v1
qinao.oracle.k3.provider-recovery-action.v1 -> qinao.oracle.invariant.provider-recovery-action.v1
qinao.oracle.k3.provider-terminal-event.v1 -> qinao.oracle.invariant.provider-terminal-event.v1
qinao.oracle.k4.max-cost-warrant.v1 -> qinao.oracle.invariant.k4-max-cost-warrant.v1
qinao.oracle.k4.provider-action-grant.v1 -> qinao.oracle.invariant.k4-provider-action-grant.v1
qinao.oracle.k4.rebase-grant-reservation.v1 -> qinao.oracle.invariant.k4-rebase-grant.v1
```

Every row predicate consumes the signed case binding/facts plus the exact `absent | present(canonicalState)` before/after preimages. All permit `absent -> absent`, a valid initial `absent -> present`, or `present -> present`; none permits `present -> absent`. Equal present bytes require equal revision. Any changed present state requires a checked strictly greater revision; the exact case-fact schedule independently derives the required increment, including every intermediate owner write, so an unaccounted skip, wrap, or revision-only churn fails. A creation has the positive revision derived from that same write schedule rather than an unconditional `1`, because one observed amendment transition may contain several governed internal writes. The field-coverage rule is closed: every declared state field not explicitly authorized to change by that row's predicate is byte-identical across present states; every authorized change has an exact signed-input projection and edge rule; and every creation field is projected from signed input/facts or a specified derivation. The verifier enumerates the descriptor's complete field set, subtracts the predicate's exact change set, and enforces equality—there is no unmentioned mutable field. Golden vectors mutate every state field one at a time, recompute all state/outcome/catalog roots, re-sign with the trusted test key, and still require failure unless that exact transition is admitted. These common rules are part of every predicate's golden table, not an implicit helper that either implementation may omit. The nine predicate bodies are closed as follows:

1. `attempt-rebase-control`: `rebasePending -> headInstalled -> activationPending -> usable|terminalDenied`, with the direct checked edges `headInstalled -> usable|terminalDenied` also allowed and terminal phases immutable. Boot/policy/deletion/revocation epochs never change; the active head changes from the signed predecessor to the signed successor at most once; successor is absent before that edge and exact afterward; `attemptGeneration` equals the signed predecessor generation before installation and the exact checked signed successor generation afterward, with the one permitted delta fixed by case facts; cumulative use is checked-subtracted exactly once; visited state is preserved; and rights, ceilings, and deadline equal the signed attenuated projections and never widen.
2. `budget-use-monotonic`: generation/epochs and the key-selected subject never change; all token/unit/reserved-cost counters are nondecreasing under checked UInt64 arithmetic; `observedCost` moves absent-to-one-exact-value at most once; `reservedMaxCost` never falls; terminal follows `open -> closed|indeterminate`; a closed/indeterminate row cannot reopen; and no receipt or state transition creates a negative/downward credit. `latestBudgetUseReceiptDigest` and `visitedStateDigest` are derived fields, not arbitrary evidence hashes. The signed case facts select exactly one of `initializedNoUse` or `receiptBearing`. `initializedNoUse` is legal only on the initial all-zero/open/observed-absent state and requires an absent latest receipt. Every governed use, reservation, observation, terminal, or later write is `receiptBearing` and requires `latestBudgetUseReceiptDigest = present(SHA256("QINAO-K3-BUDGET-USE-RECEIPT-V1\0" || uint64be(len(receiptPreimageBytes)) || receiptPreimageBytes))`, where the closed `receiptPreimage` contains exactly schema ID, canonical key and key digest, case-binding digest, absent-or-present complete before-state projection excluding the two derived fields, complete after-state projection excluding those fields, previous receipt variant/digest, checked counter deltas, observed-cost transition, terminal transition, before/after revision, and the exact non-empty declaration-ordered governed-event sequence independently projected from signed case facts. On creation the previous receipt is absent; thereafter it must equal the before row's latest receipt.

   The same event sequence drives `visitedStateDigest`. For initial creation it equals `SHA256("QINAO-K3-BUDGET-VISITED-INIT-V1\0" || uint64be(len(visitPreimageBytes)) || visitPreimageBytes)` over exact key digest, case-binding digest, and that event sequence (which is the singleton initialization event for `initializedNoUse`). For every changed present row it equals `SHA256("QINAO-K3-BUDGET-VISITED-STEP-V1\0" || beforeVisitedStateDigest || uint64be(len(stepPreimageBytes)) || stepPreimageBytes)`, where `stepPreimage` contains the same case-binding digest, checked revision edge, and exact newly visited event sequence used by the receipt. An unchanged row preserves both fields byte-for-byte; a changed row cannot reuse either old digest. The predicate recomputes both hashes from preimages and rejects a caller-supplied digest, empty/duplicate/reordered event, receipt/visited schedule disagreement, or valid re-sign after changing either field.
3. `provider-recovery-action`: the `notApplicable`/`applicable` variant is fixed by the signed applicability input and never flips. N/A is immutable except its one creation. Applicable phase follows `prepared -> pending -> armed -> handedOff -> sentOrUnknown`, with `handedOff|sentOrUnknown -> authenticatedTerminal|indeterminateQuarantined`; terminal phases are immutable. Materialized request is exact; grant/budget/observation references move absent-to-one-value and never clear/change; owner/policy/deletion/revocation epochs are fixed; action kind and ordinal remain key-bound.
4. `provider-egress-marker`: `egressKind=customExact` admits only `customTransportTaskCreatedFalse|True`; `egressKind=pccOpaque` admits only `pccRespondEnteredFalse|True`; a present row never uses `notApplicable`. The sole transition is matching-family `False -> True`; true never clears, family never changes, and epochs remain fixed.
5. `provider-terminal-event`: terminal follows `open -> failed|authenticatedTerminal|sentOrUnknown|indeterminateQuarantined`, plus `sentOrUnknown -> authenticatedTerminal|indeterminateQuarantined`; terminal results are immutable. `ownerEpoch` is exact from the signed case input and never changes. `open` has absent reason/outcome; every non-open state has the exact typed reason/outcome digest. Publication follows `blocked -> nonpublishable|publishable`; `publishable` iff `authorityReceiptDigest` is present, while blocked/nonpublishable require it absent. Proposal/result IDs are absent-to-one-value only. Generation, successor count, observed byte counts, and mechanism-start count never decrease, and each exact delta is independently fixed by the case-fact schedule; zero-send cases require all four governed zero/nil constraints, not merely a terminal enum.
6. `profile-token-observation`: `pending -> accepted|rejected`, terminal dispositions immutable. Classification is byte-exact and exhaustive: `uint` is an integer-form lexeme without `-` whose magnitude is `<= UInt64.max`; `negative` is an integer-form lexeme with `-` whose magnitude is `<= UInt64.max`; `overflow` is an integer-form lexeme whose magnitude is greater; `nonInteger` contains a fraction or exponent. Only `uint` parses into `parsed(U)`; every other class requires `invalid(tokenLexemeDigest)`, where `tokenLexemeDigest = SHA256("QINAO-K3-TOKEN-LEXEME-V1\0" || uint64be(len(exactLexemeBytes)) || exactLexemeBytes)`. A class/lexeme mismatch fails. Accepted requires the parsed value to be at most both `safeIntegerBound` and the migration-state-selected `validatorCeiling`; before migration that ceiling is the exact old hard validator bound, while after migration it is the exact newly sealed profile-specific validator bound rather than an implicit old global limit. Invalid, overflow, negative, non-integer, or over-bound input is rejected. Bound values are immutable and may not be silently widened inside a row transition.
7. `k4-rebase-grant`: grant identity, K3 anchor, epoch floors, and the signed attenuated rights/ceilings/deadline never change. Lifecycle follows `reservedInactive -> activationPending|usable|terminalDenied|revoked|expired`, `activationPending -> usable|terminalDenied|revoked|expired`, and `usable -> revoked|expired`; terminal states never reopen and a grant is never usable before the matching K3 winning head.
8. `k4-provider-action-grant`: grant/materialized-request identity, K3 anchor, action key, time-domain/deadline, and epoch floors never change. The signed `GT` tag determines observation presence exactly. `reservedInactive` has all three ticks absent. `success` requires `claimedAtTick = present(claim)`, `spentAtTick = present(handoff)`, terminal observation absent, and `claim <= handoff <= deadline`. `expiredBeforeClaim` requires lifecycle expired, claim/spent absent, terminal observation present and `expiry > deadline`; `expiredAfterClaim` requires the signed claim present, spent absent, terminal observation present, `claim <= deadline < expiry`. The two revoked variants use the analogous presence shape and a current revocation-fence trace event; `terminalDenied` has only its signed terminal observation and reason-bearing trace event. Lifecycle follows `reservedInactive -> claimed|terminalDenied|revoked|expired`, `claimed -> spent|terminalDenied|revoked|expired`; terminal states never reopen, and at most one claim/spend exists for the exact action/ordinal. A claim/handoff after a terminal observation, cross-domain tick, deadline widening, missing/extra tick for the selected tag, absent expiry/revocation trace, or assertion of expired while `observedTick <= deadlineTick` fails.
9. `k4-max-cost-warrant`: the six reserved components use checked addition and their exact sum equals `reservedMaxCost`; `downwardCredit` is exactly zero in every state; a present observed cost never changes the reservation. Warrant/profile/price/K3-anchor identities and quota/platform bounds remain fixed. Lifecycle follows `reservedInactive -> active|terminalDenied|revoked|expired|indeterminate`, `active -> spent|revoked|expired|indeterminate`; terminal states never reopen.

The registry's six declaration-ordered `amendment_relation_laws` are exactly these records and subject tuples:

```text
qinao.oracle.relation.a1.strict-rebase.v1 =
  amendment=A1 roles=[case-input,rebase-control,rebase-budget,rebase-grant,oracle-outcome]
qinao.oracle.relation.a2.finite-recovery.v1 =
  amendment=A2 roles=[case-input,cancel-recovery,query-recovery,cancel-budget,
  query-budget,cancel-grant,query-grant,egress-marker,terminal-event,oracle-outcome]
qinao.oracle.relation.a3.same-boot-owner-loss.v1 =
  amendment=A3 roles=[case-input,terminal-event,oracle-outcome]
qinao.oracle.relation.a4.pcc-pre-entry.v1 =
  amendment=A4 roles=[case-input,pcc-marker,terminal-event,oracle-outcome]
qinao.oracle.relation.a5.max-cost-no-credit.v1 =
  amendment=A5 roles=[case-input,max-cost-budget,max-cost-warrant,oracle-outcome]
qinao.oracle.relation.a6.profile-token-bound.v1 =
  amendment=A6 roles=[case-input,profile-token-budget,profile-token-observation,oracle-outcome]
```

The role arrays are the fixed predicate vocabulary; the signed case/wave derivation below decides which row instances are due, so a W2 A1 case does not fabricate a W5 grant and an A2 N/A action does not fabricate a budget/grant. Their executable semantics are likewise closed. A1 joins the same workspace/work-unit/turn/predecessor/successor/request/lease across its due rows, requires exactly one checked active-head winner, preserves visited state/cumulative use, and requires the due W5 grant to remain inactive until that K3 winner and to carry the same attenuation/epochs. A2 makes the signed applicability variants the sole row-cardinality source and requires each variant's proof digest to equal its recovery row: N/A actions have recovery proof rows but no budget/grant; applicable actions have the exact same proof/action/request/ordinal budget and due W5 grant; cancel precedes query when both apply; and no lost/uncertain result creates a second start. Its marker must match `egress` exactly—`customExact` can never join a PCC marker and `pccOpaque` can never join a custom marker—and the terminal/outcome publication, ID, byte, start-count, and attestation projections must equal the owner rows. A3 permits exactly the terminal-event role and a zero-successor failed transition only for signed same-boot `.inProcessCertified`, fenced-dead owner, nil K/B/X/O/S, closed boundaries, current epochs, and containment proof; it admits no budget/grant/marker row. A4 fixes egress to PCC, requires the PCC marker family, and permits zero-send failure only from definitely false pre-entry marker under the uninterrupted same owner; true/unknown/owner-loss is `sentOrUnknown`. A5 joins the same turn/attempt/branch/lease/warrant/profile/price/bounds, requires both rows to carry the identical checked six-component maximum, and requires zero downward credit regardless of lower/missing/unknown observed cost. A6 joins every profile/geometry/request/lease/grant identity, hashes the exact numeric lexeme into invalid state when parsing fails, and accepts only a valid parsed UInt64 within the safe bound and the signed current validator ceiling; before migration that is the old hard ceiling, while after the atomic migration the new sealed profile ceiling may lawfully exceed the old value without retaining it as a hidden global cap.

For every due A1/A2/A5/A6 budget role, the relation predicate independently reconstructs the complete BudgetUse receipt and visited-event preimages from that amendment's exact joined request/action/warrant/profile row plus signed case facts. A1 uses the same rebase event schedule as the control row and requires its cumulative-use transition to equal the budget counter deltas. A2 uses the action-specific proof/request/materialization/grant/terminal sequence separately for cancel and query and forbids swapping the two receipt chains while leaving valid keys. A5 binds the receipt's reserved components, observed transition, and terminal event to the warrant row. A6 binds its receipt/visit event to the exact profile-token observation lexeme/class/disposition. Thus a revised budget row with changed `latestBudgetUseReceiptDigest` or `visitedStateDigest`, a receipt from another role/case, or a validly re-signed catalog with recomputed outer roots still fails the corresponding relation law. Golden tables contain one single-field re-signed negative vector for each derived digest and one cross-role/cross-case substitution for every due budget role.

For each applicable A2 action, the grant row must repeat the AP's materialized request and exact `GT`-selected time domain/deadline/observation presence and values. The production trace binds those observations to the grant-reservation, claim, expiry/revocation/denial, handoff, and action events. A success-tag claim occurs only while the exact one-shot grant is reserved/unexpired and handoff/spend occurs only after claim and no later than the deadline. Before-claim expiry has no claim/handoff; after-claim expiry preserves claim but has no handoff; both force the typed denial/expired outcome with no mechanism start after terminal observation. Cancel/query grants have independent timing lineages and cannot exchange a still-valid grant. The relation rejects a fresh-looking grant digest with stale timing, wrong GT tag, missing/extra observation, clock-domain substitution, claim/handoff after expiry, expiry asserted without its trace event, deadline changed after reservation, or K3 action progression beyond the K4 lifecycle.

Each relation predicate deterministically projects **every** `OracleOutcomeV1` field from the signed input/facts and validated rows, not only `rowDeltas`. A1 alone may carry the rebase `activeHead`/generation/successor projection; A2/A3/A4 mirror the exact terminal row's terminal/publication/proposal/result/byte/start projections; A5/A6 require those unrelated projections and `activeHead` to use their amendment-specific signed `notApplicable` reason. `publicationState.publishable(authorityReceiptDigest)` is legal only when the same terminal row is publishable with that identical present receipt; blocked/nonpublishable requires its row receipt absent. `rejectedNoMutation` requires byte-identical complete pre/post aggregates; any mutation requires the case-fact-selected non-rejection disposition, and an idempotent/race disposition must reopen the exact signed winner. Generation/successor deltas equal checked differences of the due rows and cannot be caller-selected. Thus an otherwise shape-valid top-level field cannot escape the row/relation laws.

For each row invariant, `golden_vector_table_digest = SHA256("QINAO-K3-ORACLE-ROW-INVARIANT-GOLDENS-V1\0" || uint64be(len(invariantIDBytes)) || invariantIDBytes || uint64be(len(canonicalVectorTableBytes)) || canonicalVectorTableBytes)`. For each relation law the same grammar uses domain `QINAO-K3-ORACLE-RELATION-LAW-GOLDENS-V1\0` and its law ID. A vector entry is exactly `{vector_id,case_binding,state_preimages,outcome,expected_verdict,expected_error_code}`; tables sort by `vector_id`, contain at least one positive vector for every legal edge and one single-clause negative vector for every sentence above, and are embedded independently in the Task-2 admission verifier and post-embed verifier tests. The registry stores only the digests, but admission requires byte-equality with those code-owned tables. At minimum, validly rehashed and trusted-test-key re-signed mutations for `customExact + pccRespondEntered*`, `publishable + absent authorityReceiptDigest`, `downwardCredit = 7`, and `accepted + parsed value above the applicable bound` must fail solely under these predicates.

`OracleCaseBindingV1` closes case inputs, logical keys, and atomic transaction partition before expected state is authored. The catalog carries `case_bindings[165]` sorted by unsigned-UTF-8 `case_id`; each entry has exactly `case_id`, `amendment_id`, `fixture_id`, `fixture_class`, `realization_wave`, `suite_id`, `case_input_schema_id`, `case_input`, `allowed_reason_codes` sorted by unsigned UTF-8, `row_key_bindings[] = {row_role,row_scope_schema_id,canonical_key,key_digest}` sorted by `(row_role,row_scope_schema_id,key_digest)`, and `transaction_partition`. All three row sort-key fields are required; both `row_role` and `(row_scope_schema_id,key_digest)` are independently unique within a case, so the aggregate/delta join cannot collapse two roles onto one logical row. Adjacent transposition fails even when object-key canonicalization and set membership remain unchanged. Every identity field is cross-derived from the plan constants and later byte-compared with the ledger. `case_input_schema_id` is `<full-amendment-id>.case-input.v1`; its exact all-required/no-extra object fields are:

| amendment | Exact case-input fields |
|---|---|
| A1 | `workspaceScopeDigest:D, workUnitDigest:D, turnOperationDigest:D, predecessorAttemptDigest:D, successorAttemptDigest:D, rebaseRequestDigest:D, reservationRequestDigest:D, attemptLeaseDigest:D, bootSessionDigest:D, policyEpoch:U, deletionEpoch:U, revocationEpoch:U, caseVariant:V, caseFacts:F` |
| A2 | `turnOperationDigest:D, attemptDigest:D, branchDigest:D, originalPermitDigest:D, leaseDigest:D, cancelApplicability:AP, terminalQueryApplicability:AP, providerProfileDigest:D, egress:EG, terminalEventDigest:D, ownerEpoch:U, policyEpoch:U, deletionEpoch:U, revocationEpoch:U, caseVariant:V, caseFacts:F` |
| A3 | `turnOperationDigest:D, attemptDigest:D, branchDigest:D, terminalEventDigest:D, bootSessionDigest:D, ownerEpoch:U, policyEpoch:U, deletionEpoch:U, revocationEpoch:U, containmentProofDigest:D, caseVariant:V, caseFacts:F` |
| A4 | `turnOperationDigest:D, attemptDigest:D, branchDigest:D, terminalEventDigest:D, providerProfileDigest:D, ownerEpoch:U, policyEpoch:U, deletionEpoch:U, revocationEpoch:U, caseVariant:V, caseFacts:F` |
| A5 | `turnOperationDigest:D, attemptDigest:D, branchDigest:D, leaseDigest:D, warrantRequestDigest:D, profileDigest:D, priceRevisionDigest:D, reservedInputCost:U, reservedOutputCost:U, reservedReasoningCost:U, reservedToolCost:U, reservedCacheCost:U, reservedRequestCost:U, reservedMaxCost:U, observedCost:O<U>, quotaBoundsDigest:D, platformBoundsDigest:D, caseVariant:V, caseFacts:F` |
| A6 | `turnOperationDigest:D, attemptDigest:D, profileDigest:D, geometryDigest:D, materializedRequestDigest:D, leaseDigest:D, grantDigest:D, observedTokenLexeme:S[json-number-lexeme-v1,128], observedTokenClass:E[uint,negative,overflow,nonInteger], safeIntegerBound:U, validatorCeiling:U, migrationState:E[beforeAtomicMigration,afterAtomicMigration], caseVariant:V, caseFacts:F` |

For A2, `cancelApplicability` and `terminalQueryApplicability` are exactly `notApplicable(applicabilityProofDigest) | applicable(applicabilityProofDigest,actionRequestDigest,materializedRequestDigest,grantRequestDigest,grantTiming:GT)` under the universal tagged-union wire. The proof digest is mandatory in both variants and the relation predicate requires the corresponding recovery row's `applicabilityProofDigest` to be byte-identical; it cannot be state-authored or inferred from the action request. `GT` fixes exactly which time observations exist: success carries claim+handoff; before-claim expiry/revocation carries only its terminal observation; after-claim expiry/revocation carries claim+terminal observation; terminal denial carries its observation and governed reason. All ticks use the same signed monotonic time domain and deadline, and a N/A action carries no timing payload. `egress` is `notApplicable(reasonCode) | customExact | pccOpaque`; the latter two carry no payload because `providerProfileDigest` is already mandatory. A4 implicitly fixes `egress = pccOpaque`. These values are normative input facts reviewed independently of output, not implementation observations. Mutations remove/substitute the applicable proof or timing tag/value while keeping every action/request key valid and must fail.

`transaction_partition` is a signed, independently derived atomicity contract, not implementation output. It is exactly `{schema_version:1,groups,partition_digest}`. `groups[]` is sorted/unique by `semantic_group_id`; each group is exactly `{semantic_group_id,members,distinct_from_group_ids}`. `members[]` is non-empty, sorted/unique by `(row_role,phase,boundary_kind,occurrence_ordinal)`, and each member has exactly those four fields; `distinct_from_group_ids` is the sorted complete set of every other group ID. `partition_digest = SHA256("QINAO-K3-CASE-TRANSACTION-PARTITION-V1\0" || uint64be(len(canonicalPartitionWithoutDigest)) || canonicalPartitionWithoutDigest)`. The Task-2 constants expand these fixed templates after removing phases/roles not due in that case: A1 has `pending`, atomic `head+budget`, `grantReserve`, and `grantActivateOrDeny`; each applicable A2 action independently has atomic `prepare+budgetReserve`, `grantClaim`, `arm`, `grantSpend`, `handoff`, and atomic `terminal+budgetClose`, plus a separate marker/start group when due; A3 has `containmentTerminal`; A4 has distinct `markerFence` and `terminalization`; A5 has distinct `budgetReserve`, `warrantReserve`, `warrantLifecycle`, and `budgetObservationClose`; A6 has atomic `tokenObservation+budgetObservation`. A `+` is one group; commas name pairwise-distinct groups. Every signed extra failed/CAS attempt becomes its own `semantic_group_id + ".attempt." + occurrenceOrdinal` singleton immediately before its target group.

Every transaction-tagged attempt/change event for the case maps to exactly one member; all members of one group carry the same decoded production `transaction` identity and all distinct groups carry unequal IDs. A committed-change member's local participation set is derived from `touched_physical_keys`; a failed/CAS-attempt member's is derived from its separate `attempted_physical_keys` even though its mutation/touched aggregate remains empty. The identity/native receipt's covered-source array is the exact sorted union of those local participation sets; each local set is retained in its invocation, may differ from a sibling's set, and is a subset of that union. Decision/denial/serializer events use the fixed per-kind N/A literal above, query events use observation identities outside this partition, and no catalog or producer may merge or split groups. Cross-owner roles never appear in one `+` group. The parent reconstructs the partition, local participation sets, and group union from amendment constants, decoded requests, due-row derivation, signed case facts, writer counts and phases before reading observed IDs, then requires full member/event/union equality. Golden mutations split `head+budget` or `tokenObservation+budgetObservation`, merge two distinct A2/A5 phases, change one N/A literal, drop/add one attempted/touched/union source, reuse a caller label over two native receipts, or preserve final rows/order while changing only the partition; all fail.

`caseVariant` is not an opaque digest. For a multi-case override it is exactly that bracket label; otherwise it is exactly `fixtureID + ".default"`. `K3_AMENDMENT_CASE_FACTS_V1` is a reviewed 165-entry constant keyed by `(case_id,caseVariant)`. Each value is a non-empty exact array of `{fact_id,fact_value}` sorted by `(fact_id,canonicalFactValueBytes)` under unsigned UTF-8/byte ordering; `fact_id` is unique within the case, so a tie is itself invalid rather than an alternative order. `fact_value` uses only `bool(B) | uint(U) | digest(D) | enum(factSchemaID,value)` under the universal tagged wire, and every `fact_id/factSchemaID/value` comes from the amendment-specific closed allowlist in the verifier. The A3 allowlist explicitly and separately covers Provider kind, same/cross boot, owner `fencedDead|absent|unknown|live`, K/B/X/O/S nil/present, all five boundary states, Attempt/generation/policy/deletion/revocation currentness, normal-terminal CAS schedule, and orphan-Artifact status. A1 covers transition checkpoint/use/replay/CAS/attenuation/visited-state/boot/activation; A2 covers action ordering/applicability/ordinal, GT time-domain/deadline/claim/handoff/expiry/revocation/denial schedules, grant/budget/observation/marker/start/race/owner/ID/byte/attestation axes; A4 covers PCC marker/crash/entry/error/owner/ID/byte/attestation axes; A5 covers usage/cancel/timeout/uncertainty/overflow/refund/bounds; A6 covers every identity currentness, numeric class, decoder bound, migration phase, and silent-widening axis. The external binding contains these fact preimages, and the Task 2 verifier requires exact equality with its independently reviewed constant. Missing/extra/substituted facts, an adjacent swap, an enum value from another axis, or a valid re-sign over easier facts fails; no decisive predicate may hide behind `caseVariant` or a digest.

`K3_AMENDMENT_ORACLE_REQUIRED_ROW_SCHEMAS_V1` is a deterministic key-derivation function over that signed input and fixture wave. A1 derives roles `rebase-control` and `rebase-budget` from its request/lease fields, plus `rebase-grant` at W5 from the same predecessor/successor/reservation request. A2 always derives `cancel-recovery`, `query-recovery`, and `terminal-event`; applicable cancel derives `cancel-budget` and, at W5, `cancel-grant`; applicable terminal query derives `query-budget` and, at W5, `query-grant`; `egress-marker` is due only at W5 when `egress` is custom/PCC. These four action budget/grant tokens are distinct exact `row_role` strings, never a generic role plus hidden action metadata. A governed-not-applicable action therefore has a recovery applicability witness but no fabricated BudgetUse/grant identity. A3 derives only `terminal-event`; A4 derives `pcc-marker` plus `terminal-event`; A5 derives `max-cost-budget` plus `max-cost-warrant`; A6 derives `profile-token-budget` plus `profile-token-observation`.

The key projection is exact rather than inferred from those role names:

```text
A1 rebase-control = (workspaceScopeDigest,workUnitDigest,predecessorAttemptDigest,rebaseRequestDigest)
A1 rebase-budget = (turnOperationDigest,attemptDigest=predecessorAttemptDigest,
  leaseDigest=attemptLeaseDigest,budgetSubject=attemptRebase,subjectOrdinal=0,
  subjectRequestDigest=rebaseRequestDigest)
A1 rebase-grant@W5 = (predecessorAttemptDigest,successorAttemptDigest,reservationRequestDigest)
A2 cancel/query-recovery = (turnOperationDigest,branchDigest,originalPermitDigest,
  actionKind=cancel|terminalQuery,ordinal=0)
A2 cancel-budget = (turnOperationDigest,attemptDigest,leaseDigest,
  budgetSubject=providerCancel,subjectOrdinal=0,
  subjectRequestDigest=cancelApplicability.actionRequestDigest)
A2 query-budget = (turnOperationDigest,attemptDigest,leaseDigest,
  budgetSubject=providerTerminalQuery,subjectOrdinal=0,
  subjectRequestDigest=terminalQueryApplicability.actionRequestDigest)
A2 cancel-grant@W5 = (turnOperationDigest,branchDigest,
  actionKind=cancel,ordinal=0,
  grantRequestDigest=cancelApplicability.grantRequestDigest)
A2 query-grant@W5 = (turnOperationDigest,branchDigest,
  actionKind=terminalQuery,ordinal=0,
  grantRequestDigest=terminalQueryApplicability.grantRequestDigest)
A2 egress-marker@W5 = (turnOperationDigest,branchDigest,providerProfileDigest,
  egressKind=customExact|pccOpaque)
A2 terminal-event = (turnOperationDigest,branchDigest,eventDigest=terminalEventDigest)
A3 terminal-event = (turnOperationDigest,branchDigest,eventDigest=terminalEventDigest)
A4 pcc-marker = (turnOperationDigest,branchDigest,providerProfileDigest,egressKind=pccOpaque)
A4 terminal-event = (turnOperationDigest,branchDigest,eventDigest=terminalEventDigest)
A5 max-cost-budget = (turnOperationDigest,attemptDigest,leaseDigest,
  budgetSubject=maxCostWarrant,subjectOrdinal=0,subjectRequestDigest=warrantRequestDigest)
A5 max-cost-warrant = (turnOperationDigest,branchDigest,warrantRequestDigest,
  profileDigest,priceRevisionDigest)
A6 profile-token-budget = (turnOperationDigest,attemptDigest,leaseDigest,
  budgetSubject=profileTokenObservation,subjectOrdinal=0,
  subjectRequestDigest=materializedRequestDigest)
A6 profile-token-observation = (attemptDigest,profileDigest,geometryDigest,
  materializedRequestDigest,leaseDigest,grantDigest)
```

Every derived key uses only immutable facts available **before** the possible row creation. The A1/A2/A5 K4 keys use reservation/action/warrant request digests; minted `grantDigest`/`warrantDigest` lives only in present state. Budget keys use the closed subject enum, ordinal `0`, and the matching rebase/action/warrant/materialized-request digest, never an opaque catalog-selected subject. The verifier constructs the canonical key objects and digests itself, then requires byte equality with `row_key_bindings`; the catalog cannot choose another 64-hex key. It rejects an input/key mismatch, an absent action with budget/grant key, an applicable action missing either due key, a minted ID embedded in a key, wrong action/ordinal/profile/request join, too-early schema, or foreign amendment.

Let `caseBindingBytes` be the canonical bytes of one complete entry. `caseBindingDigest = SHA256("QINAO-K3-ORACLE-CASE-BINDING-V1\0" || uint64be(len(caseBindingBytes)) || caseBindingBytes)`. Let `caseBindingArrayBytes` be the canonical complete 165-entry array. `caseBindingRoot = SHA256("QINAO-K3-ORACLE-CASE-BINDING-SET-V1\0" || uint64be(len(caseBindingArrayBytes)) || caseBindingArrayBytes)`. Each eventual ledger `cases[]` record stores the matching `case_binding_digest`; the signed selection, catalog, Owner Ledger amendment records, runner receipt, and pre-admission receipt all carry `case_binding_root`. Aggregate pre/post row keys and `rowDeltas` must equal the selected case binding exactly; neither the outcome catalog nor candidate implementation defines its own key set.

Let `registryBytes` be the canonical bytes of that complete registry. `oracleCellSchemaRoot = SHA256("QINAO-K3-ORACLE-ROW-SCOPE-REGISTRY-V1\0" || uint64be(len(registryBytes)) || registryBytes)`. For a row scope, `ownerID` must equal the selected entry's `authority_owner_id`, `rowKind` must equal its `logical_row_kind`, `canonicalKeyBytes` is the canonical JSON of the exact key object after registry validation, and `keyDigest = SHA256("QINAO-K3-ORACLE-ROW-KEY-V1\0" || uint64be(len(rowScopeSchemaIDBytes)) || rowScopeSchemaIDBytes || uint64be(len(canonicalKeyBytes)) || canonicalKeyBytes)`. A caller-supplied digest, owner/kind alias, field-order-dependent encoder, SQLite-affinity coercion, `NULL`, float, decimal string, omitted field, unknown enum, or digest without its key preimage fails. This is a logical evidence encoding; a later physical mapper must explicitly convert its SQL/BLOB/actor values into these types and may not reinterpret them as the physical storage encoding.

A closed `StateFixtureV1` entry contains exactly `state_fixture_id`, `state_scope`, and `canonical_state`. `state_scope` is either `aggregate(fixtureID)` or `row(rowScopeSchemaID,ownerID,rowKind,canonicalKey,keyDigest)`. A row entry's `canonical_state` is the exact closed state object for that registry entry. An aggregate entry's `canonical_state` is exactly `{fixture_id, rows[]}`, where every sorted row is `{rowScopeSchemaID,ownerID,rowKind,canonicalKey,keyDigest,value}` and `value = absent | present(stateFixtureID,canonicalStateDigest)`. Its row multiset must equal the independently derived required multiset above; pre/post aggregates for one outcome have byte-identical logical keys and may differ only in `value`. Every present value resolves exactly one matching row fixture. Let `canonicalStateBytes` be the canonical bytes of the complete entry, including its ID, scope, key preimage, and state. `canonicalStateDigest = SHA256("QINAO-K3-ORACLE-STATE-FIXTURE-V1\0" || uint64be(len(canonicalStateBytes)) || canonicalStateBytes)`.

Every outcome's `rowDeltas` is non-empty and is mechanically derived by pointwise joining the complete pre/post aggregate rows on `(rowScopeSchemaID,keyDigest)`. It must equal that derived list byte-for-byte and repeats both key preimage and digest; it therefore includes rejected/unchanged evidence as `before == after` and creations/deletions as explicit absent/present transitions. Neither catalog authors nor implementations may omit a stable witness, add an unscoped row, or use an opaque aggregate digest to hide a key. Every `before/after.present` reference must resolve to the same row schema/owner/kind/key and recomputed state digest. Thus neither a digest without its preimage, a whole-state/row-state substitution, nor an internally signed but semantically arbitrary JSON state is legal.

`expectedOutcomeDigest = SHA256("QINAO-K3-ORACLE-OUTCOME-V1\0" || uint64be(len(outcomeBytes)) || outcomeBytes)`. The closed catalog object contains exactly `schema_version = 1`, `oracle_canonicalization_id = qinao-k3-oracle-outcome-json-v1`, `oracle_cell_schema_root`, `oracle_row_scope_registry`, `case_binding_root`, sorted `case_bindings[165]`, `state_fixture_catalog_root`, sorted `state_fixtures[]`, and sorted `outcomes[165] = {case_id, expected_oracle_id, outcome}`. Let `stateFixtureBytes` be the canonical bytes of the complete `state_fixtures` array and `catalogBytes` the canonical bytes of that complete catalog object. `stateFixtureCatalogRoot = SHA256("QINAO-K3-STATE-FIXTURE-CATALOG-V1\0" || uint64be(len(stateFixtureBytes)) || stateFixtureBytes)`, `catalog_sha256 = SHA256(catalogBytes)`, and `oracleCatalogRoot = SHA256("QINAO-K3-ORACLE-CATALOG-V1\0" || uint64be(len(catalogBytes)) || catalogBytes)`. The embedded registry and bindings must recompute to `oracle_cell_schema_root` and `case_binding_root`. `state_fixtures[]` must equal the **minimal transitive closure** reached from all 165 outcome pre/post aggregate references and each aggregate's present row references: no unresolved, duplicate, unreachable, or orphan fixture is legal. Python and Swift independently recompute every registry/binding/key/state/outcome/catalog digest and root from reviewed preimages.

For each outcome, the pre-edit Task-2 admission verifier first derives `(owningPlannedFixtureID, owningPlannedFixtureClass, owningAmendmentID, realizationWave, permittedRowOwnerIDs)` from its independently reviewed `K3_AMENDMENT_IDS_V1`, binding-group, case-ID, decision-phase, and owner constants—never from the not-yet-created candidate ledger. After embedding, the distinct post-embed verifier derives the same tuple from the stage-0 candidate Ledger plus `CANONICAL_DECISION_PHASES_V1`, requires exact equality with the pre-admission selection/receipt, and rejects a ledger edited to make a bad catalog self-consistent. `OracleOutcomeV1.fixtureClass` must equal the derived closed enum exactly, both aggregate scopes must name the derived fixture ID, every schema must be permitted for the derived amendment/wave, and each registry authority owner must be in `permittedRowOwnerIDs`. A signature never grants a foreign row authority. Missing/ambiguous case ownership, `acceptingExpected`↔`rejecting`, cross-fixture aggregate substitution, foreign owner, unknown schema, wrong key cardinality, or a schema introduced before its realization wave fails before transition comparison.

At W0 the logical registry proves expectations only. In the applicable W2/W5 candidate, the authority-owning current/earlier `ExtensionSlice` members reached by each controlled fixture's exact required-edge closure carry the closed `oracleRowRealizations[]` availability projection defined in the ExtensionGate schema above. Each record names the exact registry root/schema ID, authority owner, identical storage owner, versioned physical sources, complete field/conversion mapping, writer symbol, transaction/snapshot boundary, and independently implemented physical-to-logical key/state serializer symbol. Its schema fixture proves every physical column/field/type/NULL/BLOB/integer rule maps to the frozen logical descriptor and that the globally collapsed **revision chain** has no fork or second active writer/mapper. Duplicates of the same `(rowScopeSchemaID,mappingRevision)` on qualifying members must be byte-identical; different revisions are legal only through the exact predecessor/activation/backward/replay rules above, and the highest complete active revision is selected globally before a case selects its signed schema/key subset. A due mapping that is absent, same-revision inconsistent, chain-forked, partially migrates a shared source, many-to-one ambiguous, placed on a foreign owner or primary-only member, points at the frozen Rust EventLog/schema-only K4/another owner, or changes the logical schema/root blocks that wave. A genuinely insufficient logical schema requires a new independently reviewed registry/catalog version and new oracles; a future slice cannot silently widen v1.

The Swift observed-outcome serializer reads the actual transaction change receipts and scoped before/after owner snapshots through those admitted mappings, independently constructs the complete logical aggregate and deltas without reading expected outcomes, and requires exact object equality with the selected oracle. Before a schema's realization wave it cannot be required; at or after that wave, “table absent,” “mapper unavailable,” a catalog-driven omission, or a fabricated absent row is a hard failure. This removes the W0→future-`ExtensionSlice` circular dependency without pretending physical implementation exists.

Before Task 4, an independent reviewer must supply canonical `qinao-k3-case-oracle-selection-v1` bytes containing exactly the approved-spec tuple, `case_id_set_root`, `oracle_canonicalization_id = qinao-k3-oracle-outcome-json-v1`, `oracle_cell_schema_root`, `case_binding_root`, `state_fixture_catalog_root`, `oracle_catalog_root`, `catalog_sha256`, `source_baseline_selection_sha256`, `source_import_admission_sha256`, `reviewer_trust_root_sha256`, the complete signed `execution_intent`, `execution_intent_sha256`, repeated `implementer_key_id`, `implementer_principal_id`, `implementer_role = w0-implementer`, `implementer_epoch`, `selection_issued_at_utc`, `admission_python_id`, `admission_python_path_commitment`, `admission_python_real_path_commitment`, `admission_python_sha256`, `admission_python_version`, `admission_python_implementation`, `admission_python_stdlib_closure_root`, `admission_verifier_version = qinao-k3-oracle-admission-v1`, `admission_verifier_commit`, `admission_verifier_tree`, `admission_verifier_source_path = scripts/check_qinao_external_oracle_admission.py`, `admission_verifier_source_blob`, `admission_verifier_source_sha256`, `admission_verifier_test_path = scripts/test_check_qinao_external_oracle_admission.py`, `admission_verifier_test_blob`, `admission_verifier_test_sha256`, `reviewer_key_id`, `reviewer_principal_id`, `reviewer_role = oracle-reviewer`, `reviewer_epoch`, `signature_algorithm`, and `signature`. `source_import_admission_sha256` is raw SHA-256 of the frozen importer-produced canonical receipt. The seven Python fields are exact repeats of the nested Task-0-signed commit-safe `admission_python` identity and closure, not reviewer-selected values; raw local paths remain only in the external execution environment. `case_id_set_root = SHA256("QINAO-K3-CASE-ID-SET-V1\0" || uint64be(len(canonicalSortedCaseIDArray)) || canonicalSortedCaseIDArray)`. `selection_issued_at_utc` uses the same canonical UTC-seconds grammar, is no earlier than the execution intent's issue time, and lies within that intent's validity interval. The verifier commit is the unique Task-2 commit that first introduced the standalone admission verifier/test and exact golden predicates; its tree/path/blob/raw hashes are reconstructed from Git and neither frozen path may have a later parent-edge touch before admission. The trusted key fingerprint/principal/role/epoch comes from the same Task-0-admitted offline trust-root allowlist whose exact bytes/digest and unique source-selection digest are compiled into the Task-2 verifier. Both key and principal must differ from the nested intent's implementer; a key/root declared only inside this record is never trusted. Signature verification covers `"QINAO-K3-ORACLE-SELECTION-V1\0" || uint64be(len(unsignedSelectionBytes)) || unsignedSelectionBytes`, where the unsigned bytes are the same closed canonical object with only `signature` removed; the source-baseline selection/trust-root binding, nested signed intent, algorithm/key/principal/role/epoch, complete Python/frozen-verifier identity, issue time, and every root remain covered. The exact domain literal is independently golden-tested; no prefix alias is accepted.

The registry, 165 case bindings/outcomes, and all referenced state fixtures are independently authored from the normative table/case projection—not generated by Python/Swift implementation output—and the selection signature covers the case set/bindings, row-scope schema, state-fixture, catalog, verifier, and full-byte digests/roots. Task 4 persists both inputs under the K3 addendum's unique `### Controlled Oracle Catalog — qinao-k3-case-oracle-catalog-v1` heading. Its bounded body contains exactly two `json` fences in order: first the signed selection object, then the closed catalog containing the registry and bindings; no third fence, duplicate heading, intervening equal-or-shallower heading, or prose substitute is legal. Fence extraction removes exactly the one Markdown structural LF adjacent to each closing delimiter and otherwise preserves bytes; each extracted object must already be canonical with no trailing LF.

Mutations independently alter the physical-baseline empty allowlist, registry/schema ID, descriptor kind/field/enum/order, allowed amendment or realization wave, authority owner, logical row kind, invariant/law ID/subject/predicate version/golden digest, canonical key byte, key digest/length/domain, state-field byte/type, aggregate key set/cardinality, row-delta derivation, state-reference digest, outcome digest, every root, and raw catalog digest. The suite additionally transposes adjacent case bindings/facts/row bindings/invariants/laws; adds one valid but unreachable state fixture; flips `acceptingExpected`↔`rejecting`; swaps valid pre/post states between fixtures; substitutes a foreign owner/schema/key; removes/adds one actual delta; violates each row phase/revision/internal constraint; violates each A1–A6 join; or changes a registry descriptor/law and then recomputes every dependent digest/root and re-signs with the trusted test key. Python and Swift must still fail solely on their independent pinned registry/fact/predicate constants, case-owner/class/wave, required-row multiset, minimal fixture closure, permitted owner, physical realization, or independently observed exact-set inequality. Missing/untrusted/expired selection, implementer=self-reviewer, unresolved/orphan state fixture, ambiguous fence, observed-derived expectation, shared expected/observed builder, or Task-2/post-embed verifiers sharing a semantic predicate implementation blocks staging with `BLOCKED_ORACLE_ROW_SCOPE_UNAVAILABLE`.

The Owner Ledger first adds exactly one closed top-level `controlled_oracle_admission` record with exactly `schema_version = 1`, `admitted_at_utc`, `admission_python_id`, `admission_python_path_commitment`, `admission_python_real_path_commitment`, `admission_python_sha256`, `admission_python_version`, `admission_python_implementation`, `admission_python_stdlib_closure_root`, `source_baseline_selection_sha256`, `source_import_admission_sha256`, `reviewer_trust_root_sha256`, `execution_intent_sha256`, `implementer_key_id`, `implementer_principal_id`, `implementer_role`, `implementer_epoch`, `reviewer_key_id`, `reviewer_principal_id`, `reviewer_role`, `reviewer_epoch`, `selection_sha256`, `catalog_sha256`, `case_id_set_root`, `oracle_cell_schema_root`, `case_binding_root`, `state_fixture_catalog_root`, `oracle_catalog_root`, `admission_verifier_version`, `admission_verifier_commit`, `admission_verifier_tree`, `admission_verifier_source_path`, `admission_verifier_source_blob`, `admission_verifier_source_sha256`, `admission_verifier_test_path`, `admission_verifier_test_blob`, `admission_verifier_test_sha256`, `execution_commit`, `execution_tree`, `task4_controlled_path_set_root`, and `admission_receipt_sha256`. The time is copied exactly from `GatewayOracleAdmissionBodyV1` and must satisfy the nested intent/selection issue/expiry interval; the last value is raw SHA-256 of the complete authenticated gateway envelope. The post-embed checker reconstructs every payload field from Git, embedded signed intent/selection/catalog, fixed counts/pins, exact candidate path set, source-import admission, and Task-0 interpreter/verifier commitments; then it verifies the envelope's canonical wrapper, parent Accepted receipt, signature/key/epoch, scope/global journal inclusion, matching Completed receipt, payload equality, and raw digest. CI later revalidates the stored original time against the signed window rather than consulting wall clock. A caller-written identity/time/receipt field, a raw machine path, a digest copied without an authenticated envelope, or a ledger/catalog pair edited together fails. Mutations alter `admitted_at_utc`, either path commitment, wrapper parent/scope/signature, or executing identity and must fail even when every candidate-owned digest is recomputed.

Task 4 extends only the normal owner-checker parser with one all-or-none, read-only comparison quartet: `--execution-intent <external> --admission-selection <external> --admission-catalog <external> --admission-receipt <external>`. It is legal only with normal `--root --ledger --through`; any partial quartet is a parse error. The checker opens all four files under the same no-follow/mode/link rules, requires the execution-intent bytes to equal the complete nested object in the first K3 fence, requires selection/catalog byte identity with both bounded K3 fences, validates both signatures and key/principal separation, requires the exact current staged path tuple to equal the signed twelve-path set, requires the receipt to equal the reconstructed canonical success bytes, and then performs ordinary stage-0 ledger validation. CI/cumulative runs omit the quartet and instead validate the persisted nested intent, selection signatures, known path-set constant, commit history, and committed receipt digest; omission never disables catalog, identity separation, signature, predicate, or ledger validation.

The same atomic Ledger edit adds exactly one top-level `controlled_amendments` array with six ordered, closed records and no other amendment registry. Each record has exactly `amendment_id`, the complete five-field `approved_spec` object, `document_path`, `exact_heading`, `section_digest`, `slice_ids`, `oracle_canonicalization_id`, `oracle_cell_schema_root`, `case_binding_root`, `state_fixture_catalog_root`, `oracle_catalog_root`, and `planned_fixtures`. Each non-empty `planned_fixtures[]` member has exactly `fixture_id`, `fixture_class = acceptingExpected | rejecting`, `realization_wave`, sorted non-empty `required_slice_ids`, `realization_slice_id`, `suite_id`, and sorted non-empty `cases`. Each `cases[]` member has exactly `case_id`, `case_binding_digest`, `expected_oracle_id`, and `expected_outcome_digest`; `case_id` is sorted/unique and `expected_oracle_id` is deterministically `caseID + ".oracle.v1"`. There is no fixture-level oracle and no flat `case_ids` side list: the reviewed catalog maps every pair to that exact signed input binding, ID, closed outcome, and independently recomputed digest. Missing fields use explicit typed variants, never omission/defaulting; two cases with different durable outcomes cannot share an oracle ID or outcome digest. The checker rejects a missing/extra/default binding/oracle, Boolean-only outcome, pair collision, schema/binding/state/catalog-root drift, or field mutation. `document_path` is always `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-k3-budget-provider-contract-addendum-design.md`; all headings/digests/slices/fixture/class/oracle/case/binding fields are duplicate-key/canonical-order checked. The derived accepting/rejecting ID projections must equal the two table columns exactly, so there is no second flat source of truth.

`K3_AMENDMENT_FIXTURE_DEPENDENCY_CLOSURES_V1` prevents a fixture from naming only its easiest K3 leaf while omitting the contract/profile/material authority that makes the proof meaningful. `through(Wn, decisions=[...])` expands, in canonical `(wave,decisionID,ownerID)` order, to **every** `CANONICAL_DECISION_PHASES_V1` slice for each named decision whose phase wave is `<= Wn`; an empty decision expansion, unknown decision, or caller-added/removed slice fails. The exact closures are:

```text
D1W2=through(W2,decisions=[attempt-rebase])
D1W5=through(W5,decisions=[attempt-rebase,attempt-rebase-grant])
D2W2=through(W2,decisions=[plan-provider-selection,provider-profile-variants,provider-recovery-action])
D2W5=through(W5,decisions=[context-accounting,plan-provider-selection,provider-profile-variants,provider-recovery-action,remote-materialization-defer])
D3W2=through(W2,decisions=[plan-provider-selection,provider-profile-variants,provider-recovery-action])
D4W5=through(W5,decisions=[plan-provider-selection,provider-profile-variants,provider-recovery-action])
D5W5=through(W5,decisions=[certified-context-geometry,certified-operating-envelope,context-accounting,k4-material-authorization,plan-provider-selection,provider-profile-variants,remote-materialization-defer])
D6W2=through(W2,decisions=[certified-context-geometry,certified-operating-envelope,context-accounting,plan-provider-selection,provider-profile-variants])
```

For every binding group, `required_slice_ids` is derived only from its named closure and must contain the primary plus every implementation slice listed for that amendment whose phase wave is at or before the group's realization wave. The checker separately requires the union of all binding-group closures for one amendment to cover every implementation slice in that amendment's table row, including later-wave slices; it never requires a W2 fixture to depend on an unrealized W5 slice. It asserts exact per-group equality, not subset sufficiency, and mutation-removes every earlier contract phase and every cross-decision owner one at a time. This specifically forces A1/A2/A3 to bind their W1 contract phases, A6 to bind both W1 geometry/certification producers and its plan/profile/accounting lineage, and W5 remote/PCC/cost fixtures to bind the complete earlier profile/material lineage. A closure may be deliberately narrowed only by a future reviewed version of this constant and new catalog outcomes; a fixture/caller cannot narrow it.

`K3_AMENDMENT_FIXTURE_BINDING_GROUPS_V1` is the following exact compact golden projection. `A1...A6` alias the six amendment IDs in table order; ordinal lists select full `.accept.NN`/`.reject.NN` IDs. `closure` selects the exact dependency expansion above; the primary realization slice must be a member of that expansion. An ordinal sub-list may be empty, but every expanded fixture has non-empty `cases`. The checker expands these groups to individual closed `planned_fixtures` records and rejects overlap or an uncovered table fixture. A caller-written symbol is intentionally absent: only placement on the actual primary ExtensionGate member at realization time selects the exact symbol/path/version.


Each bracket entry becomes the checker-side human label for the fixture's ordered `.case.NN`; aliases never enter ledger JSON. Every unlisted fixture has exactly `.case.01`, and no parser may silently default a listed compound family back to one case. The projection expands to exactly **86 fixtures and 165 cases**; tests derive both counts and exact sets rather than trusting the numerals. W0 records and validates these planned identities, sections, bindings, cases, and pair-specific typed oracles only: W0 executes zero production schema fixtures and cannot claim realization. In the applicable W2/W5 wave, exactly one nested member in the primary `ExtensionSlice` lists the fixture ID in `realizedPlannedFixtureIDs`, while that outer primary envelope lists all planned `(fixture_id, required_slice_id)` values in `realizedPlannedFixtureEdges`. The current cumulative runner revalidates every named earlier/current ExtensionSlice from one candidate snapshot, reruns the unique fixture suite, and embeds the sanitized child receipt. That child reports every `fixture_id/case_id`, exact primary/required edge projection, non-zero matched count, the complete observed `OracleOutcomeV1`, and its exact minimal observed state-fixture preimage closure—**no expected Oracle ID/digest and no observed digest field**. Only after the child returns does the parent aggregate select and inject `expected_oracle_id/expected_outcome_digest`, independently compute the observed digest from the complete observed object, and compare full canonical objects; those parent fields are not child-receipt fields. Across the active manifest and parent aggregates, realized IDs/edges/cases/oracles must equal the planned set exactly; early, missing, duplicate, foreign-amendment, wrong-wave/slice/suite/member/case/oracle, or wrong-outcome realization fails.
